Website Critique Assignment 
Submitted by: Sakshi Desai 
Company: Eragina Digital Solutions 
Website Reviewed: https://www.doright.world
1. Design Review
The overall design of the website lacks visual appeal.
  
The color scheme is dull and not vibrant, which makes the content less engaging.
  
The navigation bar only has two buttons, which makes the website feel incomplete 
and confusing.
The images used are not attractive and do not capture attention or reflect the brand 
effectively.
2. User 
Experience
 Review
The website is simple, but it does not offer a smooth and interactive experience for
users. 
Important sections like “About Us,” “
Contact
,” or “Services” are missing from the 
top menu.
There are no clear call-to-action (CTA) buttons to guide users.
  
The content feels disconnected and does not highlight the purpose of the website 
well.
3. Suggestions for Improvement
Use a more vibrant and modern color scheme to improve visual engagement.
  
Add more navigation options such as “About,” “Services,” and “
Contact
” for better 
usability.
Replace current images with high-quality and brand-relevant visuals.
  
Add clear CTA buttons like “Learn More,” “Get Involved,” or “Join Us” to guide
user flow. 
Improve the homepage layout with well-defined sections and headlines to make it 
easier for visitors to understand the website’s mission.
Thank you!

Projects

Detailed Critique on DoRight website
Upon reviewing the website, I noticed the following things:
1. The first thing which I observed was the color palette
and typography which is consistent throughout the site which makes it look more professional and highlights the brand identity.
2. Even though the first impression of the website is usually the color theme and look of the site, the content also plays a crucial role in grabbing the attention of the visitors. The website lacks in detail information related to their clients and successful projects, including which would result in more registrations of new clients.
3. Although the colour palette of the website is consistent it can use more of visual elements to make the website more attractive and engaging for the users.
4. The website can also incorporate SEO to improve the
site's visibility in search engine results.
5. The site performs well on mobile devices, making it
easier for users to access the website according to their convenience.
6. The subtle effects like animation and hovering on the buttons and links makes the site more interactable.
7. The search bar feature is missing from the website which
would make it easier to access.
8. Overall the website is fine, but it can definitely use more colour and visual elements to make it more impressive.

OMS Interview Preparation Guide
Technical Readiness + How to Answer (Confidence-Safe)
Purpose of this document 
This guide is meant to organise what you already know, not teach you new 
things or give scripted answers. 
Use it as a mental checklist so your answers are clear, structured, and 
aligned with what OMS typically looks for.
1. How OMS Interviews (Reality Check)
OMS interviews are project-centric and practical.
They are trying to understand: - Have you built real systems end-to-end? - Do you 
understand workflows, validation, and reliability? - Can you explain your work clearly to 
other engineers?
They are not testing: - DSA / algorithms - Framework trivia - Overly deep 
distributed-systems theory
2. How to Structure Any Technical Answer (Very Important)
Whenever you are asked about any feature, module, or project, mentally structure 
your answer in this order:
1. Context – What problem was being solved? 
2. Responsibility – What did you work on? 
3. Approach – How was it designed at a high level? 
4. Reliability / Validation – How did you ensure correctness? 
5. Outcome – What improved or changed?
You do not need to explicitly label these in the interview — just ensure they are covered.
3. Project Discussion (Core of the Interview)
What OMS Expects
You should be able to explain one major project confidently, end-to-end.
They will probe: - Business workflow - API flow - Validation & error handling - One real 
issue you fixed
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
1
Private & Confidential
When Explaining a Project, Make Sure You Cover
What kind of system it was (workflow-driven, compliance-driven, internal users, 
external users)
How the frontend and backend interact
 • 
Where business logic lives
 • 
How failures are handled
Avoid jumping straight into code or frameworks.
4. Backend (Spring Boot) — What to Be Clear About
You should be comfortable explaining:
Architecture
Controller → Service → Repository separation
 • 
Why business logic belongs in services
APIs
How REST endpoints are designed
 • 
How request validation is done
 • 
How errors are returned to the frontend
Error Handling
Centralised exception handling
 • 
Meaningful error responses (not stack traces)
 • 
Logging for debugging
Security (High Level)
Authentication vs authorization
 • 
Basic Spring Security / JWT concepts
You are not expected to explain internal Spring mechanics.
5. Frontend (Angular) — OMS Level Expectations
OMS expects clean, practical Angular usage, not deep framework internals.
Be clear on: - Component vs service responsibilities - Form handling and validation - 
API integration - Error handling in UI - Basic performance practices
When answering Angular questions: - Keep explanations simple - Focus on user 
experience and correctness
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
2
Private & Confidential
6. Database & Data Handling
What They Look For
Sensible schema / collection design
 • 
Correct data validation
 • 
Pagination for large datasets
 • 
Basic indexing awareness
If asked about MongoDB: - Explain why document databases are useful for 
workflow-heavy systems - Show conceptual clarity even if hands-on exposure is limited
Avoid deep database internals.
7. Reliability, Debugging & Production Issues
You should be ready to discuss one real issue you handled.
When describing a problem: 1. What went wrong? 2. How did you detect it? 3. How did 
you debug it? 4. What change fixed it?
OMS values engineers who can keep systems stable, not just build features.
8. Communication & Presentation Tips
Speak calmly and at a steady pace
 • • •
If a question is broad, pause and structure your answer 
It’s okay to ask for clarification 
If you don’t know something deeply, explain what you do know clearly
Avoid: - Over-explaining internals - Jumping between topics - Using too many 
buzzwords
9. Final Mental Checklist
Before each answer, quickly check: - Am I explaining the problem first? - Did I mention 
my responsibility? - Did I cover correctness and reliability? - Is my answer clear and 
concise?
If yes, you’re answering at the right level for OMS.
10. OMS Tech Readiness Checklist (Last-Mile Prep)
Use this section as a confidence check, not a study syllabus.

What you changed - What improved after

project (Acacia or AFRI) and explain it end-to-end

Workflow steps - Validation points - Data correctness and auditability

OMS Interview Preparation Guide
Technical Readiness + How to Answer
Purpose of this document 
This guide is meant to organise what you already know, not teach you new 
things or give scripted answers. 
Use it as a mental checklist so your answers are clear, structured, and 
aligned with what OMS typically looks for.
1. How OMS Interviews (Reality Check)
OMS interviews are project-centric and practical.
They are trying to understand: - Have you built real systems end-to-end? - Do you 
understand workflows, validation, and reliability? - Can you explain your work clearly to 
other engineers?
They are not testing: - DSA / algorithms - Framework internals - Deep distributed- 
systems theory
2. How to Structure Any Technical Answer (Very Important)
Whenever you are asked about any feature, module, or project, mentally structure 
your answer in this order:
1. Context – What problem was being solved? 
2. Responsibility – What did you build or own? 
3. Approach – How was it designed at a high level? 
4. Reliability / Validation – How did you ensure correctness? 
5. Outcome – What improved or changed?
Avoid starting answers with framework internals.
3. Project Discussion (Core of the Interview)
What OMS Expects
You should be able to explain one major project clearly, end-to-end.
They will probe: - Business workflow - Frontend → Backend → Database flow - Validation 
& error handling - One real production issue you fixed
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
1
Private & Confidential
Power Access H2H (Axis Bank) or Sahara (Govt workflow system)
When explaining the project, ensure you cover: - Who the users are - What workflow the 
system supports - How Angular and Spring Boot interact - How failures are handled
4. Backend (Spring Boot) — What to Be Clear About
You should be comfortable explaining:
Architecture
Controller → Service → Repository separation
 • 
Why business logic belongs in services
APIs
REST endpoint design
 • 
Request validation (bean validation, checks in service layer)
 • 
Error responses and HTTP status codes
Error Handling
Global exception handling
 • 
Custom exceptions
 • 
Logging for debugging
Security (High Level)
Authentication vs authorization
 • 
JWT-based security flow (conceptual)
Avoid deep dives into Spring filter chains unless explicitly asked.
5. Frontend (Angular) — OMS Level Expectations
OMS expects clean, user-focused Angular usage.
Be clear on: - Component vs service responsibilities - Form handling and validation - 
API integration using services - Showing loaders and error messages - Preventing 
double submit
When answering UI questions: - Start with user flow - Mention error handling - Keep 
explanations simple
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
2
Private & Confidential
6. Database & Data Handling
What They Look For
Correct data modeling
 • 
Validation at backend
 • 
Pagination for large datasets
 • 
Indexing (high level)
If asked about MongoDB: - Explain when document DBs are useful - Show conceptual 
understanding - Focus on schema design and pagination
7. Reliability, Performance & Debugging
You should be ready to discuss: - One performance improvement (caching, pagination, 
pooling) - One production bug you fixed
When explaining an issue: 1. What went wrong 2. How you detected it (logs / 
monitoring) 3. What you changed 4. How you prevented recurrence
Avoid listing too many tools; focus on reasoning.
8. Communication & Presentation Tips
 • 
Pause before answering broad questions
 • 
Do not jump into internals immediately
 • • 
Keep answers structured 
It’s okay to say you’ll check logs or involve seniors
Avoid: - Overusing buzzwords - Explaining internals unless asked
9. Final Mental Checklist
Before each answer, check: - Did I explain the problem first? - Did I clarify my 
responsibility? - Did I mention validation and error handling? - Is my answer clear and 
concise?
10. OMS Tech Readiness Checklist (Last-Mile Prep)
Backend – Spring Boot
Be confident explaining: - Layered architecture - DTO vs Entity - Validation and custom 
exceptions - Global exception handling - Basic JWT security flow
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
3
Private & Confidential
REST APIs & Data Flow
Be able to walk through: - One API end-to-end - Error handling and status codes - 
Pagination
Frontend – Angular
Be comfortable with: - Forms and validation - API calls via services - Loaders and error 
messages - Preventing duplicate submissions
Database (MongoDB / SQL)
Be clear on: - When to use MongoDB vs SQL - Indexing (conceptual) - Pagination
Reliability & Debugging
Prepare one clear story: - Bug or performance issue - Root cause - Fix - Prevention
11. Focus Areas Based on Screening Discussion
The following emphasis areas are based on your screening conversation and are 
meant to improve clarity, not change your experience.
Answer Structure (High Priority)
You tend to start with internal framework flow. Instead: - Start with the business 
problem - Then explain high-level flow - Mention internals only if asked
Spring Boot Internals
You clearly understand Spring internals. For OMS: - Keep explanations at architectural 
level - Avoid deep filter-chain or servlet lifecycle descriptions unless requested
Angular Error Handling
Ensure you explicitly mention: - Loaders during API calls - User-friendly error messages
 - 
Disabled submit to prevent duplicates
Database & Performance Positioning
You have worked with: - Pagination - Redis caching - Connection pooling
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited
4
Private & Confidential
When explaining performance: - Focus on why changes were made - Avoid tool-heavy 
explanations
Strengths to Lean Into
Make sure you surface these clearly: - Workflow-heavy projects (Banking, Govt 
systems) - Production debugging experience - Performance optimization (pooling, 
pagination) - CI/CD exposure
Closing Note
If you explain your work calmly, starting from the problem and ending with reliability, 
you’ll be well aligned with OMS expectations.
Eragina Digital Solutions Private Limited

Private & Confidential
